# Live Git Indexing (`engram` / cluster-mesh / h2a)

Spec: `spec/SPEC_LIVE_GIT_INDEXING.md` (cited **LIVE §x**). Base: `c9ed019331d1ea0fbf96890cca602fd5f6817088`; S0 branch: `feat/live-git-indexing-s0`. Owner decisions, dated 2026-10-11: **Q1 A · Q2 B · Q3 A · Q4 A · Q5 A · Q6 A · Q7 A**, plus D under L3a qualification and its accepted D → B / Q7 A → B alternatives (LIVE §1). **L3a v4.1 supersedes the older implementation split:** cluster-mesh owns the generic kernel and both reusable SQLite/Postgres backends; the app injects and operates Postgres. Live produces no versioned file and writes nothing into observed worktrees.

**2026-10-17 read-side amendment:** [LIVE — Read side](../spec/SPEC_LIVE_GIT_INDEXING.md#read-side-2026-10-17) records the accepted owner decisions and analysis A–D. The read side is the equivalent of Scion (Anthropic, *Redesigning source control*, October 2026) on top of Git, which stays the version authority. Δ identifiers below amend existing lots, without adding a separate Scion project or execution lots. Realize the gaps natively; git-ai and its proposed comparative trial are excluded. The 20-case protocol fixes continuation at ≥30% median gain; an execution pilot requires ≥20% additional gain. External-model disclosure requires explicit repository/provider/scope/duration authorization, with secret exclusions and default denial. All added implementation/test/measurement obligations remain unchecked and `unverified`.

**2026-11-19 commissioning amendment:** the owner validated dossier v6 on 2026-11-15/16 with option 1 (open the complete vertical slice up to the `/code-git` readers first, then run the performance qualifications and the 20 real investigations) and its 6-step completion sequence. On 2026-11-19 the owner required that the specification and the plan of the commissioning lots be validated by Fable 5.2 and Carbon before any builder starts (English rendering of the owner's wording, not a verbatim quotation). Both validations returned NO-GO on the plan at `f75543a5`; this amendment applies their findings. [Commissioning Order](#commissioning-order) replaces the former Recommended Order, [Commissioning Rules](#commissioning-rules) fixes the prerelease rule, builders, disk floors and shared-file sequencing, and [Validation 19/11 — dispositions](#validation-1911--dispositions) records one line per finding. Audited heads: E `f75543a5`, S `bb3f3cefb`, H `947f7d325`. A line anchor written `plan/09:N` in the ledger refers to this file at `f75543a5`, before this amendment.

**2026-11-19 round-2 amendment:** the second validations (`VALIDATE-golive-fable-r2.md` and `VALIDATE-golive-carbon-r2.md`, same ignored design directory) again returned NO-GO, with no blocker: one buildability major on L5-P2 raised by both validators, one on E-ASM1 raised by Carbon, and 22 minors. This amendment re-specifies L5-P2 from its measured import closure and splits it into rows 7a and 7b, removes the type defect that stopped E-ASM1, applies every minor, and records one line per finding in [Validation r2 — dispositions](#validation-r2--dispositions). The row numbers of round 1 are kept so that both validation tables stay readable: rows 4 and 5 are swapped, and a split or added row takes a letter suffix (7a, 7b, 35a, 35b, 42b). In the round-2 ledger an anchor written `plan/09:N` refers to this file as amended in round 1 (1,010 lines), which is the numbering both round-2 validations use.

**2026-11-19 round-3 amendment (final fold):** the third validations (`VALIDATE-golive-fable-r3.md` and `VALIDATE-golive-carbon-r3.md`, same ignored design directory) returned GO-WITH-MINORS for Fable 5.2 (no blocker, no major, two minors on L5-P2a and three observations) and NO-GO for Carbon on one major and nothing else: R3-01, a file missing from the Allowed list of E-ASM3, for which Carbon gave the fix and stated that it converts its verdict to GO. Owner decision of 2026-11-19 (English rendering of the owner's wording, not a verbatim quotation): GO with the correction and the cleanup; complete the assignment of lots by difficulty, so that Neon codes what has to be coded by Neon, Hydrogen what is simpler, and Ember where possible. This fold applies R3-01 by Carbon's own fix, applies both minors and the three observations, assigns a builder to every lot by difficulty ([Commissioning Rules](#commissioning-rules), “Builders”), and records one line per finding in [Validation r3 — dispositions](#validation-r3--dispositions). Row numbers are unchanged, and no lot is added or removed. Heads read by the round-3 validations: E `origin/main` `f751f8d6`, one commit after the audited `f75543a5`; S `bb3f3cefb`, unchanged; H `origin/main` `e9ce1a8db`, 33 files after the audited `947f7d325`. Every anchor of this plan holds at the head it names, and the receiving-branch rule covers the drift. In the round-3 ledger an anchor written `plan/09:N` refers to this file as amended in round 2 (1,164 lines), which is the numbering both round-3 validations use.

**Cited specifications.** **LIVE §x** is `spec/SPEC_LIVE_GIT_INDEXING.md`. **SPEC_L3A §x** and the phrase “L3a v4.1” both name the frozen `spec/SPEC_L3A_GENERIC_MODULE_STORAGE_PORT.md`; its §17.4 file names are proposals, not delivered paths. **Q1–Q7** keep the single meaning of LIVE §1 (the 2026-10-11 dossier identifiers: Q1 materialization, Q2 local content, Q3 fetch scope, Q4 activation, Q5 storage exception, Q6 Postgres publication, Q7 SQLite runtime). The register of dossier v6 reuses the labels Q1–Q7 for a different seven-row summary; builders never read a Q label with that second meaning.

Paths prefixed **E:** belong to engram, **S:** to sentropic (including `packages/cluster-mesh`), **H:** to h2a. Unprefixed test cases use the named repository. New paths are proposed, not existing APIs. **Each external lot has its own owning-repository branch; cross-repository lots have one scoped branch per repository.** Allowed lists below are exact source files, not directory wildcards; generated install/build/test outputs are ephemeral. Migration numbers are reserved against the audited base and must be replaced by an exact scope amendment if occupied when the receiving branch starts. L3b means L3b-S/L3b-P, L7b means L7b-C/L7b-R/L7b-D, and L9 means L9-E/L9-S/L9-H. Since round 2, L5-P2 means L5-P2a then L5-P2b, and L7a means L7a-A then L7a-C: a dependency written “L5-P2” is a dependency on L5-P2b, and a dependency written “L7a” is a dependency on L7a-C. No external implementation is hidden in S0.

## Objective

- [ ] Deliver automatic, hosted, deterministic code/Git indexing after one explicit workspace activation, with uncapped committed history, automatic GitHub PR synchronization, filesystem watch, separate index/working-tree captures and explicit coverage (LIVE §§1–12).
- [ ] Keep engram a neutral fact producer in the separate `@sentropic/engram-live` package; cluster-mesh owns adapter/port/generation semantics and both reusable SQLite/Postgres backends, while the app injects and operates Postgres (L3a v4.1).
- [ ] Deliver the read side through Δ-L5/G1, Δ-L7b-R/G2/G3, Δ-L7a/L8/G4 and Δ-L9-S/L9-H below; G5 remains conditional on measured utility in L7b-R.
- [ ] Execute one logical change per lot with at most 12 listed files per repository branch; split further before exceeding scope without dropping a gate. Lot size does not authorize an oversized commit in later implementation.
- [ ] In this S0 authoring turn, produce only the four requested English documents; no commit, runtime change, test implementation, migration, Track write or external-repository edit.
- [ ] Treat every future RED/GUARD and performance/backend/deployment result as `unverified` until executed. Q1–Q7 are accepted decisions; do not reopen them as missing approval.

## Scope

- [ ] Allowed: **S0 only:** E:`spec/SPEC_LIVE_GIT_INDEXING.md`, E:`spec/SPEC_GRAPH_STORAGE_BACKENDS.md`, E:`spec/SPEC_STORAGE_BACKENDS.md`, this plan. Later branches may touch only their lot's exact Allowed files, including the explicitly listed contracts/package/CI and app migration paths.
- [ ] Forbidden: Python files/interpreters/build fallbacks; LLM/embedding calls or assistant artifacts on live paths; implicit npm/npx/downloads; source worktree/cache/config/index/ref writes; repository-triggered programs; SQL-to-Git bridges; canonical-memory changes; engram-owned host registries; global corpus/annotation migration; general remote refresh; raw private publication; implementing additional graph backends in this delivery; `.track/**`, `BRANCH.md`, `.graphify/scratch/**` or dossier edits in S0; commits, merge, push, tag, publish or deployment in S0. Future graph backends remain supported by the backend-neutral port design.
- [ ] Conditional: exact package/build/CI files only in their owning lots; no unrelated refresh. Measured L3a failure triggers the applicable accepted alternative, conductor review, and requalification against the same thresholds. No automatic runtime fallback; return to the owner only if the alternative violates an accepted constraint. Record an exact scope amendment before adding any receiving-repository path.
- [ ] 2026-10-17 documentary scope supersedes S0-only restrictions for this amendment: only E:`spec/SPEC_LIVE_GIT_INDEXING.md` and E:`plan/09-BRANCH_LIVE_GIT_INDEXING.md`; one local documentation commit authorized, no Python, network, implementation, Track write or push. Gate: `git diff --check` and exactly these two changed paths. Future amendment Allowed lists below are separate bounded receiving branches, not permission to combine their union with a parent lot's scope.
- [ ] 2026-11-19 documentary scope (commissioning amendment): only E:`plan/09-BRANCH_LIVE_GIT_INDEXING.md` and E:`spec/SPEC_LIVE_GIT_INDEXING.md`; no commit in the authoring turn, no Python, no code or test change, no Track write. Gate: `git diff --check`, exactly these two changed paths, then a GO from both validators (Fable 5.2 and Carbon). The two storage amendments are outside this scope and are owned by lot S0-g in the order table.
- [ ] 2026-11-19 round-2 documentary scope: the same two files and the same gate as the item above, for the amendment that answers the round-2 validations; no commit in the authoring turn, no Python, no code or test change, no Track write.
- [ ] 2026-11-19 round-3 documentary scope (final fold): the same two files and the same gate, for the fold that answers the round-3 validations and applies the owner decision of the same day; no commit in the authoring turn, no Python, no code or test change, no Track write. LIVE changes in its amendment record only (one paragraph, “Round 3”).

## Commissioning Order

This table is the owner-validated 6-step sequence (dossier v6 §2.3, lines 121–128, and option 1, line 885). Rows are in build order; a letter suffix marks a row split or added in round 2. A lot starts only when every lot of its “Depends on” cell is merged with a green gate and the free-space floor of its disk class holds ([Commissioning Rules](#commissioning-rules)). A disk cell written “C, run D” means that the lot is built and merged in class C and that its full-corpus run, operated by the conductor, needs the class D floor; that run holds back no other row. “Files” is the size of the lot's exact Allowed list for one repository branch; it never exceeds 12. The RED cases, the Allowed list and the gate of a lot are defined once, in the section named in the “RED and gate” cell. Builders, assigned by difficulty in round 3 under the rule of the [Commissioning Rules](#commissioning-rules): **Neon** = `agy gemini-4-neon-xhigh`; **Hydrogen** = `agy gemini-4-hydrogen-xhigh`; **Ember** = `muse-ember-1.0` at max effort. The “Why this builder” cell gives the reason in one phrase. Repositories: **E** engram, **S** sentropic, **H** h2a.

Delivered on `origin/main` and therefore absent from the table: S0, L0, L1a, L1b, H0, L2a, L2b, R1 (E, H), P1 (E, and the H metadata transport), L4a, L4b-1, L4b-2, L5 (five classes), the Δ-L5/G1 producer, L6a (E, H, S), L6b, the Δ-G2 producer, the read-side oracles, the timing harness, and the fail-closed core of Δ-G4 in H. Their realization state is reconciled in each lot section.

Step names, in the dossier's order: **1** activate `@sentropic/engram-live` and cut its prerelease; **2** deliver the live adapter and the local vault in cluster-mesh and run the 100,000-commit corpus on SQLite; **3** open `/code-git` and its typed readers and catalogue the neutral peer; **4** assemble local supervision in h2a on the released packages; **5** activate the Postgres projection and the server guard in sentropic; **6** run the double reception (fixed thresholds on both backends, installed SLO, 20 real investigations).

Three placements follow from option 1 and are deliberate. Readers of step 3 run on SQLite only: L7b-R no longer depends on L3b-P, and Postgres parity is proven in step 5 by L3a-L3. The 100,000-commit corpus is ingested in step 2 by Q0, which is indicative; the fixed-threshold verdict Q waits for step 6, when the installed producer and host capabilities it measures exist (SPEC_L3A §17.3, Q row; §18.1, “Recommended order”); the same indicative measurement on Postgres is Q0-P, row 42b, in step 5. Δ-G3-E is built in step 1 so that the neutral package is released once with the complete G1–G3 contracts.

**Row 29 is a conditional predecessor (round 3).** Row 29 (Δ-G3-S) waits on the memory lots MCP-L1, MCP-L4, MCP-L7 and MCP-L8, which belong to another plan; their delivery state is `unknown` in this plan (`source-gap` in the Fable round-3 validation). Rows 38, 47 and 50 therefore name row 29 as a predecessor only once it is delivered. If it is not delivered when their other predecessors are green: row 47 is built and run with the reason capability reported unavailable, and the cases that need it return their explicit non-success state; row 38 stays open with row 29 and holds back no row; row 50 runs the acceptance exit, and its record lists the reason capability (G3: rows 29 and 38) as `not covered`, with the owning plan. When row 29 is delivered later, rows 29 and 38 are built in table order and reach an installed host through a further cluster-mesh release and h2a pin, cut and recorded as R-S2 and R-H2 are. The acceptance exit of this plan never waits on another plan.

## Commissioning Rules

**Prerelease rule and release checkpoints.** Each checkpoint is operated by the conductor and records version, tarball integrity (`npm pack --json` `integrity` and the SHA-256 of the tarball) and contract and fixture digests.

| Checkpoint | Content | Condition | Unblocks |
|---|---|---|---|
| **R-E-pre** | Packed `@sentropic/engram-live` under a prerelease version, never under the `latest` dist-tag | Rows 0b and 1–13 green; E-REL procedure followed | Step 2 (L3a-L1, L3a-L2, L3b-S), Q0, and an early start of L7a-V1 |
| **R-E** | Final neutral release: a re-pack of the commit of R-E-pre under the final version. Only the `version` field of the packed `package.json` differs; every other packed file is byte-identical, and the record proves it file by file | The m8 and m12 cases of L3a-L1 and the L3b-S gate pass on SQLite against R-E-pre | R-C, L7b-R, L7b-D, L7a-V2 |
| **R-C** | `@sentropic/contracts` with `/code-git` | L7b-C green; R-E cut | L7b-D |
| **R-S** | cluster-mesh with the live adapter, readers and catalogued peer; it claims `local-live` on SQLite only | L7b-D green; the m8 and m12 cases and the L3b-S gate cases green on SQLite against R-E-pre (rows 18, 24) and again on the final pin (first act of L7b-R); R-E and R-C cut | Step 4 |
| **R-H** | h2a exact pin on R-S, replacing the then-current exact cluster-mesh pin; the exact pin on R-E is already set by L7a-V2 | Inside L7a-C | Steps 5 and 6 on the host side |
| **R-S2** | cluster-mesh release that claims `product-live` on Postgres and carries the reader amendments merged after R-S (L9-S, Δ-L9-S, and Δ-G3-S when it is delivered) | Rows 41, 42, 46 and 47 green | R-H2 |
| **R-H2** | h2a exact pin on R-S2 | First act of L9-H | The installed run of L9-H |

No publication of the neutral package under a final version happens before the m8 and m12 proofs; no cluster-mesh release happens before them either. This removes the former circular gate (R-E required before L3a, L3a and L3b-S required before R-E): the prerelease unblocks storage, and the storage gates block R-S. A consumer pins a prerelease by exact version and lockfile `integrity`; a `file:` path or an import from a neighbouring checkout stays forbidden (LIVE §11). If a storage proof requires an E contract change, the conductor cuts a new prerelease and the consumers re-pin; no final version is burned.

**Promotion by re-pack (corrected in round 2).** The integrity of an npm tarball covers its `package.json`, version included, so a tarball published under a prerelease version cannot become a final version with the same digest. The prerelease version cannot be kept as the final one either: the catalogue pins its peers by ranges (S `packages/cluster-mesh/src/modules/catalog.ts:18`) that no prerelease version satisfies. R-E is therefore a re-pack: the release workflow of E-REL packs the commit of R-E-pre again, with the final version as its only changed input. The record of R-E holds both versions, both integrities, the SHA-256 of every packed file of both tarballs and their comparison: identical for every file except `package.json`, and identical for `package.json` once its `version` field is removed. Because the storage proofs ran against the prerelease digest, the first S lot that starts after R-E (L7b-R) re-pins cluster-mesh on the final version and re-runs, against it, the m8 and m12 cases of L3a-L1 and the named cases of the L3b-S gate; R-S is not cut without that record.

**Second release train.** `product-live` on Postgres is claimed by checkpoint R-S2, after the step 5 gates and after the reader amendments of step 6; the app consumes the adapter inside the S repository and does not wait for it. h2a is re-pinned on R-S2 (checkpoint R-H2, first act of L9-H), so that the installed host measured by L9-H runs the readers as amended by L9-S and Δ-L9-S, and not R-S as cut at row 30.

**Builders (round 3, assignment by difficulty).** One rule, applied to every lot: a lot goes to the simplest builder whose class covers it. **Neon** (`agy gemini-4-neon-xhigh`) builds the lots with a cross-package contract, concurrency or fencing, storage or migration, an edit of the packed-surface guard files, or tricky tests; as in rounds 1 and 2 it also builds any lot, however small, that decides a key, a credential, an authorization or disclosure boundary, a data-loss operation or a publication path. **Hydrogen** (`agy gemini-4-hydrogen-xhigh`) builds the simpler lots that stay in one package and have clear RED cases: wiring, fixtures, small adapters, documents with their tests. **Ember** (`muse-ember-1.0`, max effort) builds the simplest mechanical lots, such as an allowlist entry, a version bump, a rename or a document-only change. The Commissioning Order gives the builder of each lot and its reason in one phrase; it is the single assignment, and a “Builder:” mention in a lot section repeats it. The count is 43 lots for Neon, 9 for Hydrogen (L5-P0, L9-E, P1-S, L7a-V2, Δ-G3-H, Q0-P, Δ-L9-S, Δ-L9-E1, Δ-L9-H) and 3 for Ember (S0-g, L7a-V3, Δ-L9-E2): the 54 build lots and S0-g. A Hydrogen or Ember lot that meets a failing gate it cannot fix inside its Allowed list stops and is reassigned one class up; it does not widen its scope. The builder of a lot is never one of its reviewers. Muse 1.3 xhigh, named in rounds 1 and 2, builds no lot of this order.

**Disk.** Free space on the commissioning host is a moving value, not a planning constant. On 2026-11-19, on a filesystem `/` of 935 G, five readings were taken with no commissioning lot running: 55 G available (94 % used) when round 1 was written, then 42 G (96 %) and 52 G (95 %) during round 2, then 47 G and 52 G (95 %) during round 3, as read by the two round-3 validations; other work on the host moves the figure by about 10 G within hours. At these readings the class D floor of the four full-corpus runs is about 90 G away, which is a conductor reclaim and not a lot. Between 103 and 104 worktrees were registered (13 engram, 35 to 36 sentropic, 55 h2a). No figure of this paragraph is therefore an input of a lot: every preflight measures the live value on the filesystem of its worktree. A lot runs the preflight at its start and again before each run of its gate, and aborts below the floor of its class. When free space falls under that floor during a lot, the builder stops at the next gate boundary, removes its fixtures, caches and containers, reports the measured value, and resumes only after a conductor reclaim. Every lot removes its fixtures, caches and containers at the end, and its evidence records the peak space it used.

| Class | Lots | What consumes space | Floor of free space before start |
|---|---|---|---|
| A | Documentary lots and E lots that run focused suites only | One E worktree with `node_modules` (0.5 to 0.6 GiB each on graphify, as measured by the Fable validation), fixtures under the worktree's `.h2a/` | 5 GiB |
| B | E lots that build, pack and install the neutral tarball or run the `live-package` lane locally | Class A plus packed tarballs, an offline install tree and a worktree-local npm cache | 10 GiB |
| C | S and H lots, including real SQLite and real Postgres lanes, and the build of the Q0, Q0-P, Q and L9-H harnesses on their routine fixture tier | One S or H worktree with `node_modules` (size `unknown`; the builder measures it at branch start), Postgres 16 and 17 images and volumes, SQLite WAL files | 20 GiB |
| D | The full-corpus runs of Q0, Q0-P, Q and L9-H | 101,502-commit repositories in four object-format and ref-format combinations, the private object store, both backends populated, crash-window copies | 140 GiB (15 % of the filesystem) |

**Reclaim and serial order.** Before row 14, the first class C lot, the conductor removes merged worktrees and stale test artefacts until at least 50 GiB are free, and records the figure before and after; row 14 does not start without that record. 50 GiB is the sum of the floors of the worktrees allowed at the same time: two of class C and one of class B. At most two class C worktrees and one class B worktree exist at the same time until the class D floor holds. The class D floor is a condition of the four full-corpus runs only, never of a build. A row whose only missing condition is the class D floor does not stop the serial chain: its harness is built and merged in class C on the routine fixture tier, later rows proceed, and the conductor executes the run and records it when the floor holds. No lot runs the full CI matrix locally: a builder runs the focused commands of its gate, and the three-version matrix runs on the hosted runners (`.github/workflows/typescript-ci.yml:101-132`).

**Builder worktrees.** A builder worktree is never declared as a live root during a build, in any repository: its fixtures live under its own `.h2a/`, and an observed root that writes its own fixtures would break the whole-worktree guard of LIVE §2.

**Shared files and serial order.** Within one repository, two branches that share any Allowed file never run at the same time: the later row of the order table rebases on the earlier one. This covers the three common cluster-mesh files, E:`engram-live/contracts.ts`, E:`engram-live/facts.ts` (from its creation by L3a-E1), E:`engram-live/integration.ts`, E:`engram-live/code-analysis.ts` (rows 5, 6 and 8, and row 11 since round 3), E:`src/extract.ts` and E:`src/cache.ts` (rows 4 and 7a), E:`tests/fixtures/multi-links/writer-allowlist.json` (row 7a since round 3; work outside this plan also edits this fixture, so L5-P2a rebases its allowances on the current `origin/main` before each run of its gate), the packed-surface set below, S:`packages/cluster-mesh/src/live/readers.ts`, S:`packages/cluster-mesh/tests/live-readers.spec.ts`, S:`packages/cluster-mesh/tests/fixtures/code-git-n-n1.json`, S:`.github/workflows/ci.yml`, S:`spec/QUALIFICATION_LIVE_GIT_INDEXING.md`, H:`packages/h2a-runtime/src/gateway-host/live-host.ts`, H:`.github/workflows/ci.yml` and each `plan/09` evidence line. The “Depends on” cell lists logical dependencies; this rule adds the serial order. Branches of different repositories may run in parallel within the disk limits.

**Packed-surface set (E).** Any E lot that adds or removes an emitted module of `@sentropic/engram-live` changes four files together: `engram-live/tsconfig.json` (`include`, lines 15–23), `scripts/build-live.mjs` (`emittedFiles`, lines 183–204), `scripts/check-live-package.mjs` (preconditions at lines 482–483 and `expectedPackedPaths` at lines 502–528) and `tests/live-neutrality.test.ts` (lists at lines 26–67, 831, 1154 and 1376). The last two are the packed-surface guard files: the CI lane `live-package` runs both (`.github/workflows/typescript-ci.yml:126-132`) and rejects any emitted file outside the exact lists (`scripts/check-live-package.mjs:556`). The neutral package declares no dependency and loads only `node:` builtins and in-package relative modules (`scripts/check-live-package.mjs:469`, `:569-571`, `:592-594`); no lot adds one.

**Packed-surface rule for every lot (conductor design note, round 2).** A lot that emits or packs a new file of the neutral package lists both guard files, E:`scripts/check-live-package.mjs` and E:`tests/live-neutrality.test.ts`, in its Allowed list; it also lists E:`engram-live/contracts.ts` when it changes the state or the list of a capability. A lot that edits an already packed module without adding or removing an emitted file runs `node scripts/build-live.mjs`, `node scripts/check-live-package.mjs` and `tests/live-neutrality.test.ts` in its gate and does not list the two guard files. Two properties of the delivered guards bound what a lot may write, whatever its Allowed list: every `.ts` file under `engram-live/` outside `dist/` is scanned as package source, packed or not, and may import only in-package relative modules and `node:` builtins other than `node:module` and `node:sqlite`, with no reference to a loader factory (`tests/live-neutrality.test.ts:639-705`, `:1028-1030`); and `engram-live/contracts.ts` and its emitted files take no import at all, a type-only import included (`tests/live-neutrality.test.ts:654-662`, `:721-729`; `scripts/check-live-package.mjs:436-447`). The rule is applied to every E lot of the order in the [packed-surface audit](#packed-surface-audit-of-every-e-lot) of the round-2 ledger, not only to the lots a finding names.

**Whole-package strict check (E, round 2).** The package project compiles the modules of its `include` list and their import closure only, and the root project compiles `src/` only. At `f75543a5` the 18 unpacked modules of `engram-live/` are therefore type-checked by no project, and the test runner transpiles them without checking types. The Carbon round-2 validation ran the package compiler options over every `engram-live/*.ts` and found exactly two errors, both TS2322 in `engram-live/snapshots.ts` (`:775` and `:889`), and none in the 27 other modules; the author re-read the two loose annotations (`snapshots.ts:130`, `:742`) and the literal type they must satisfy (`engram-live/contracts.ts:2118-2120`, `:2183`, `:2253`) and did not re-run the compiler. From row 1 onward, every E lot that edits a file under `engram-live/` runs `npx --no-install tsc --ignoreConfig --noEmit --strict --target ES2022 --module NodeNext --moduleResolution NodeNext --skipLibCheck --types node engram-live/*.ts`, the explicit-file form already used by the delivered lots, and records the result. Rows 1 and 2 must show the two baseline errors and no other; row 3 (L5-P-m8) removes them; from row 3 onward the check passes with zero error. A lot whose change makes a module outside its Allowed list fail this check stops for a scope amendment; it never leaves the error for E-ASM1, which compiles those modules for the first time and may not edit an engine.

**Receiving-branch scope confirmation (S and H).** The S and H paths of lots not yet built are proposals: at S `bb3f3cefb` the delivered storage layout differs from SPEC_L3A §17.4 (six files under `packages/cluster-mesh/src/storage/sqlite/`, no `assets/storage/sqlite/` directory, Postgres assets numbered `001` to `008`). The first act of a receiving branch is to record its exact Allowed list against the receiving `origin/main` and to take the next free migration or asset number. A list above 12 files is split before any code is written; no gate is dropped by a split. Every branch that changes files under S:`packages/cluster-mesh/` may also change the three common files of SPEC_L3A §17.4 (S:`BRANCH.md`, S:`packages/cluster-mesh/package.json`, S:`package-lock.json`); they count in the 12 and the order table includes them. For app-only S lots and for H lots, the receiving repository's branch-control file is workflow metadata, written under that repository's own rules as in the delivered R1, P1 and L6a branches; a workflow that requires it inside the diff of a 12-file lot triggers a split before the branch starts.

**Gate conventions.** Every gate command uses installed tools only: `npx --no-install`, never a bare `npx`. A required backend, runtime or fixture that is missing fails the gate; it is never a successful skip. A proposed RED that already passes on the receiving base is relabelled GUARD with a discriminating mutation. The case names written in this plan are fixed: a receiving branch may add cases, and renames or removes one only through a recorded scope amendment. The three evidence records have one owner each: E:`docs/evidence/live-git-qualification.md` holds producer, oracle and timing evidence; S:`spec/QUALIFICATION_LIVE_GIT_INDEXING.md` holds publication, reader and Q evidence, is created by L8 (the first lot of the order that lists it) and is appended in table order by Q, L9-S and Δ-L9-S, while Q0 writes its own S:`spec/QUALIFICATION_MODULE_STORAGE_Q0.md`; H:`docs/evidence/live-git-qualification.md` is the single acceptance record for A01–A17 and is written by L9-H.

- MAJOR 2: each recorded launch requires a distinct Trace2 `sid`, exactly one ordered `start` / `exit` / `atexit`, terminal `atexit`, matching argv and both exit codes matching the recorded subprocess status. Regressions reject start-only/truncated evidence, either missing terminal, foreign/reused sessions, wrong status and reversed terminals; complete nonzero exits pass. Temporarily removing per-session completion/status validation made `process trace rejects start only evidence` fail (1 failure / 15 skipped), then was restored byte-for-byte.
- MINOR corrections: `unknown hash algorithm` is classified as unsupported with the SHA-256 diagnostic preserved; constructor failures dispose the new root before rethrowing. Both repros now pass, including an empty-PATH failure; the regression also cleans up its failed fixture on the RED/mutation path. Final fixture-directory check found no `l0-*` leftovers.

## H0 — Installed, Signal-Only Hooks (LIVE §7, A05/A06/A17) — engram

- [x] RED: `tests/hooks.test.ts > a missing installed executable never invokes npm or npx`.
- [x] RED: `tests/hooks.test.ts > engram-only state is recognized and grants no write permission`.
- [x] RED: `tests/hooks.test.ts > live hooks emit a bounded signal without rebuild, export or network work`.
- [x] RED (fix): `the hook-signal CLI dispatch ignores repo-controlled .env and never writes into a worktree` + `sink destinations inside a worktree are refused after symlink resolution`.
- [x] RED (fix): `third-party hooks are chained with interpreter, args, stdin and exit code preserved`.
- [x] RED (fix): `live signals resolve the effective hooks directory git executes` (config `_` dir, Husky, controlled global config refused).
- [x] RED (fix): `live hook setup refuses executables incompatible with the hook-signal dispatch`, `an oversized signal is refused instead of written`, `a hanging pinned executable cannot delay git beyond the execution budget`, `legacy hook installation distributes no npm/npx fallback`.
- [x] GUARD: `tests/hooks.test.ts > activation and live signals leave tracked untracked ignored files gitattributes and Git config unchanged`.
- [x] GUARD: `tests/hooks.test.ts > reference-transaction signals only committed transactions and explicit non-live installation remains separate`.
- [x] Implement: pin/verify the installed executable, remove implicit package resolution and discover the real hook path.
- H0 evidence (2026-10-11, `feat/live-git-h0`, base `2a9681c2`): the 6 new cases first failed with `installLiveSignals is not a function` (6 failed / 13 passed under a hermetic git config; the ambient `core.hooksPath` global breaks legacy installs on the untouched base too). Additive live path only: legacy `install()` scripts and injection-safety pins are byte-identical. GUARD mutations, each reverted: a worktree `.live-signal-probe` write failed the invariance GUARD (1 failure); accepting the `aborted` phase failed the reference-transaction GUARD (1 failure). Gate: `tests/hooks.test.ts` + `watch-hook`/`watch-rebuild-lock` suites green, `tsc --noEmit` green, `git diff --check` clean. Activation never calls an installer; independently requested live-hook setup installs only short signals, without graph attributes or merge-driver config. Live markers use injected host storage/IPC, never `.graphify/needs_update`; `.engram/` recognition grants no write permission. Preserve third-party hooks and keep explicit legacy non-live operations separate.
- H0 fix evidence (2026-10-12, review `REVIEW-H0-sol` verdict REJETÉ, 7 constats corrigés): the marker location now comes exclusively from the host — the `hook-signal` CLI dispatch runs before `loadProjectDotEnv()` so a repo-owned `.env` can never select the sink, and any sink resolving inside the observed worktree (symlinks resolved) is refused. Third-party hooks are chained as distinct executables (`<hook>.engram-live-user`): their interpreter, args and stdin are preserved and their exit code always wins (a `reference-transaction` veto survives). The live install resolves the effective hooks dir via `git rev-parse --git-path hooks` (only `.husky/_` steps up; a `<git-dir>/_` path is honored as-is); an external/global `core.hooksPath` is refused — an assumed, documented and tested limitation with zero mutation. The pinned executable must answer `hook-signal --engram-probe` (refuses `/usr/bin/true`-style and outdated binaries); signals are capped at 1024 bytes (oversized dropped) and the dispatch runs under `timeout -k 1 2` inside hooks; the legacy runtime detect no longer distributes an `npx graphify` fallback. Ten point mutations, each verified red then reverted: dispatch after `.env` load (1), sink-check disabled (2), `exit 0` instead of the third-party status (1), `sh`-forced chain (1), blanket `_` stripping (1), out-of-repo validation removed (1), npx fallback restored (2), probe disabled (1), 1 MiB line bound (1), unbounded foreground exec (2). Gate: `tests/hooks.test.ts` 27/27 + `watch-hook`/`watch-rebuild-lock` green under `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null GIT_CONFIG_SYSTEM=/dev/null`, `tsc --noEmit` green, `git diff --check` clean.
- H0 fix evidence (2026-10-12, review `REVIEW-H0-sol2` verdict REJETÉ, 2 MAJEURS corrigés): worktree containment is now canonical on both sides (`realpath` of the nearest existing ancestor, non-existing tail re-joined) with a segment-based prefix test on `path.relative` (non-empty, non-absolute, neither `..` nor `..`+separator), so a directory named `..signals` is a worktree child, not an escape; the controlled destination is the complete target — sink directory plus marker file, symlinks resolved — so a marker pointing at a tracked worktree file is refused before opening, and a symbolic-link marker is refused outright at open time (lstat before `appendFileSync`, whatever it points at). Two new RED cases, each first failing on the pre-fix code (a `..signals` relative sink emitted and created `<worktree>/..signals/post-commit.jsonl`; a symlinked marker appended into a tracked worktree file), and each killing a point mutation verified red then reverted: `startsWith("..")` restored instead of the `..`-segment test (1), marker-symlink open refusal removed (1). Gate: `tests/hooks.test.ts` 29/29 + `watch-hook`/`watch-rebuild-lock` green under `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null GIT_CONFIG_SYSTEM=/dev/null`, `tsc --noEmit` green, `git diff --check` clean.
- H0 fix evidence (2026-10-12, review `REVIEW-H0-sol3` verdict REJETÉ, 2 MAJEURS corrigés): the marker is now written only through a verified descriptor — opened with `O_NOFOLLOW` (creation only via `O_CREAT|O_EXCL`), fstat-checked on the open fd, refusing a non-regular file, `nlink > 1` (a hard link to a tracked worktree or repository file keeps the outer `realpath` and no symlink flag, so only the descriptor reveals it), or `dev`/`ino` matching any file of the observed worktree or its git directories (resolved filesystem-only: `.git` dir, gitfile target, common dir; no Git call); a sink directory whose `realpath` is inside the worktree or a git dir is refused before any mkdir, and the line is written solely via `writeSync` on that fd. Hooks-directory validation accepts equality (`pathIsInsideOrEqual`), restoring legacy `core.hooksPath = .` at the worktree root for both legacy and live installs while signal destinations keep refusing equality. Two new RED cases, each first failing on the pre-fix code (a hard-linked marker emitted and appended into the tracked file; `core.hooksPath = .` install threw "Refusing to install"), the latter also killing the point mutation `pathIsInside` restored instead of `pathIsInsideOrEqual`, verified red then reverted; the hard-link case is its own mutation (nlink guard absent pre-fix). Gate: `tests/hooks.test.ts` 31/31 + `watch-hook`/`watch-rebuild-lock` green under `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null GIT_CONFIG_SYSTEM=/dev/null`, `tsc --noEmit` green, `git diff --check` clean.
- H0 fix evidence (2026-10-12, review `REVIEW-H0-sol4` verdict REJETÉ, 1 MAJEUR corrigé): the strict symlink-aware protections (refusals, `O_NOFOLLOW`, fstat/nlink, out-of-repo sinks) apply to the live signal and its marker sinks only; the explicit legacy install keeps HEAD's purely lexical hooks-directory check, so a `.git/hooks` symbolic link to a shared hooks directory outside the repository is installable again (third-party content still appended/preserved, uninstall clean). The conductor-ratified scan bound stays: a worktree with more than 65536 files cannot be verified, the emission is refused — but the marker created by that refusal is now removed instead of lingering empty at the host sink. Two RED cases, each first failing on the pre-fix code (the shared-hooks install threw "Refusing to install"; the 65537-file emission left an empty `post-commit.jsonl`), the first also killing the point mutation canonical `pathIsInsideOrEqual` restored in `validateHooksDir`, verified red then reverted. Gate: `tests/hooks.test.ts` + `watch-hook`/`watch-rebuild-lock` green under `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null GIT_CONFIG_SYSTEM=/dev/null`, `tsc --noEmit` green, `git diff --check` clean.
- Allowed (≤5): E:`src/hooks.ts`, E:`src/paths.ts`, E:`src/cli.ts` (hook signal dispatch only), E:`tests/hooks.test.ts`, this plan.
- Forbidden: implicit hook installation; worktree cache/marker writes; attribute/config mutation during live; overwriting third-party hooks; package resolution, export or heavy rebuild inside a hook; hooks as sole truth.
- Gate: sandbox covers PATH miss, linked worktree, configured hook directory and `.engram/` only; approved signal process only. LIVE §2 inventory/digest/status guard covers activation and signaling, including initially absent `.gitattributes` and ignored/untracked paths; explicit setup is outside that baseline.

Racines = branche par défaut + fenêtre active 30 j + têtes de PR tous états + têtes de worktrees enregistrés + racines déjà ingérées, sans aucune limite d'âge ni de nombre ; le pliage suit le journal d'observations complet détenu par l'hôte (suppression et recréation sont des observations distinctes, génération incrémentée à la recréation). Compteurs consignés par le pass : aucun changement `{extraites: 0, ignorées complètes: 274, lancements: 2}` (rev-list-count 1, rev-list 1, cat-file 0, diff-tree 0) ; reprise après interruption `{réextraites: 174, ignorées complètes: 100, lancements: 4}` — la progression n'est enregistrée qu'après accusé durable, donc le 101ᵉ commit est réextrait à la reprise ; ascendance de PR conservée de 1085 commits (1160 faits, 6 lancements) au-delà du seuil de 1000 ; cycle de vie 4 racines retenues (expiry, deletion, recréation, force-push) avec parents ordonnés et appartenance dérivée. Couverture shallow/manquante explicite : frontière greffée `state "partial"`, objet absent `state "unavailable"`, les faits retenus restent interrogeables ; une racine retenue disparue empoisonne le pass en `gaps`. Mutations GUARD, chacune vue rouge puis annulée octet par octet : rafraîchir l'activité sur simple ré-observation (1), prendre l'empreinte h2a comme clé de clone (1), dériver le clone par git-dir de worktree au lieu du common-dir partagé (1). Gate : `npx vitest run tests/live-history.test.ts tests/live-git-stream.test.ts tests/live-fixtures.test.ts tests/live-neutrality.test.ts tests/live-package.test.ts` vert (39 passés, 2 sautés préexistants), `npx tsc --noEmit -p .` vert, tsc focalisé strict (`--ignoreConfig --strict --types node`) vert sur les 4 modules L2b + le test, `git diff --check` propre.

## L3a-E — Typed Facts, Batch Codec And Completion Control (LIVE §§3, 6; SPEC_L3A §§12.3, 17.4) — engram

Added on 2026-11-19. SPEC_L3A §17.3 names lot E as the neutral-contract prerequisite of L3a-L1; at `f75543a5` it is not built: `FactBatchDto.facts` is `readonly unknown[]` (`engram-live/contracts.ts:268`) and `engram-live/facts.ts`, `generation.ts` and `batch-codec.ts` do not exist. The scope of SPEC_L3A §17.4 (11 files and a branch file) omits `engram-live/index.ts`, `scripts/build-live.mjs` and `scripts/check-live-package.mjs`, which the new emitted modules require; the lot is therefore two sub-lots, each within 12 files. Both use the packed-surface set of the Commissioning Rules. Neither edits the L6a derivation in `engram-live/registration.ts`.

**L3a-E1 — typed facts and canonical batch codec.** Builder: Neon. Depends on merged L6a and on S0-g. Disk class B.

- [x] RED: `tests/live-fact-contract.test.ts > every fact kind has an exact-key typed DTO and an unknown kind or an extra key is refused`.
- [x] RED: `tests/live-fact-contract.test.ts > canonical batch encoding is byte-stable across key order and a changed fact changes the batch digest`.
- [x] RED: `tests/live-fact-contract.test.ts > a batch above 4 MiB is refused before delivery and no prefix is acknowledged`.
- [x] GUARD: `tests/live-neutrality.test.ts > facts and batch-codec are in the exact packed surface and each missing entry is rejected`.
- [x] Implement: a versioned typed union over the fact shapes the delivered producers already emit (refs and ref observations, commits and ordered parents, changed files, PR observations, retention receipts, snapshots, diffs and hunks, code analysis, line attribution, symbol correspondence, coverage and progress), replacing `readonly unknown[]`; canonical encoding and digest of a batch in `batch-codec.ts`, with algorithm-qualified OIDs, ordered parents, ref-observation identity and complete relationship endpoints, roles and versions. A producer whose emitted bytes cannot be typed without changing them is recorded as a gap for a scope amendment; it is not edited here.
- [x] Placement of the definitions (round 2): the fact DTO types, the closed union and its pure validators live in `engram-live/contracts.ts`, because that module and its emitted files take no import at all, a type-only import from `./facts.js` included (`tests/live-neutrality.test.ts:654-662`, `:721-729`; `scripts/check-live-package.mjs:436-447`). `engram-live/facts.ts` imports them from `./contracts.js`, re-exports them, and holds the fact-kind registry and the helpers that need another in-package module; `contracts.ts` never imports `facts.ts`. The three L4b shapes the snapshot producer already delivers (`LocalDiffDto`, `LocalSnapshotDto`, `LocalCoverageReportDto`; `engram-live/snapshots.ts:819`, `:976`) keep their bytes and gain no `kind` key: the validator keeps discriminating them by structure, as `validateBatchFact` does today (`engram-live/contracts.ts:487-510`), and what changes is the last branch, where a shape that matches no member of the union is refused instead of passing through.
- [x] Typing without editing a producer (round 2): the 18 unpacked modules must keep passing the whole-package strict check of the Commissioning Rules. Where a delivered producer builds its `facts` from values that the union does not accept at compile time, the lot keeps that producer compiling through the validated form (the validator narrows an input envelope to the typed batch) and records the module as a typing gap for a scope amendment. It never widens the union to `unknown`, and it never leaves a strict error in a module outside its Allowed list.
- [x] Later fact kinds (round 2): from this lot on, a fact kind is added by editing `contracts.ts` (DTO, union, validator), `facts.ts` (registry) and `tests/live-fact-contract.test.ts` (exact-key case) together, and the three are shared files under the serial rule. Each later E lot states whether it adds a kind: Δ-G3-E adds the reason anchors and L5-D may add the verified whole-file deletion, and both list the three files; L5-P-m8, L5-P1, L5-P2a, L5-P2b, E-ASM1, E-ASM2, E-ASM3 and L9-E add none, and one that finds it needs a kind stops for a scope amendment.

## L3a — Engram Adapter To Shared Module Storage (LIVE §§3, 9, 11, 13; L3a v4.1 §§12, 16–17) — engram integration team in cluster-mesh

- [ ] S follow-up, L6b m12, owned by L3a-L1 (SQLite) and L3a-L3 (Postgres): define and qualify the purge and compaction of terminal progress while keeping the receipts that idempotence needs. Reason: storage policy and transactions are outside L6b. Acceptance: under prolonged flow and across restarts and replays no work is skipped and durable progress stays bounded, proven on each claimed backend. The SQLite proof blocks R-S, not R-E-pre (Commissioning Rules).
- [ ] Authority: FROZEN L3a v4.1 governs this section and L3b-S/L3b-P. Cluster-mesh owns the generic kernel and both reusable backends; applications inject and operate Postgres. This supersedes older plan wording assigning reusable Postgres implementation to the app or extending runtime persistence as a prerequisite.
- [ ] Retained integration: L3a-Engram-Adapter, named `@sentropic/cluster-mesh/engram-live`, implements only the engram descriptor, codecs, storage adapter, generation mapping, readers and live lifecycle/transfer integration in §17.3 L1–L3. Its implementation is in cluster-mesh. No generic kernel, backend or comments implementation belongs to the engram repository.
- [ ] External ownership: §17.1 K1/K2a/K2b/K3/K4, S1/S2a/S2g/S3, P1/P2a/P2u/P2g/P2b-J/P2b-M/P2c/P3 and optional R execute in their owning cluster-mesh branches with §17.4 scopes and profile-specific proofs. R is required only for an advertised restore-witness profile. For the storage lots that live depends on and that are not delivered at S `bb3f3cefb` (K5, S1b, S2g, S3, P2g), the L3b-S and L3b-P sections of this plan give the scope, the named cases and the gate, and prevail over the §17.4 proposals.
- [ ] External comments delivery: §17.2 C0/C1a/C1b/C2/C4a/C4b/C4c/C3 and M2-W/M3-S/M4-I remain comments-owned. Apply every Exit proof in §17.2. Comments/Postgres delivery has no dependency on Q, comments/SQLite parity or R.
- [ ] Dependencies, L3a-L1 (the lot SPEC_L3A calls L1): K3 (merged), K5, S1b and S2g on SQLite, S3 for the compaction facility that the m12 case exercises (row 17, added to the order cell in round 2), and the R-E-pre prerelease carrying L3a-E1 and L3a-E2; consume the merged L6a contracts and exact declaration/clone/worktree identities. L3a-E is a separate neutral-contract prerequisite with its own section above, not additional scope of this adapter section.
- [ ] Dependencies, L3a-L2: L3a-L1 and S3, with the applicable qualified read, protection and lifecycle profiles.
- [ ] Dependencies, L3a-L3: L3a-L2 and P2g, with the applicable P2b-J/P2b-M/P2c profiles; consume the reusable Postgres implementation and destination authority. L3a-L3 belongs to step 5; nothing in steps 3 and 4 waits for it.

## L3b-S — Shared Durable SQLite And Local Migrations (LIVE §§9–10; L3a v4.1 §§14, 16–17) — cluster-mesh

- [ ] S follow-up, L6b m12, shared with L3a: S3 supplies the storage facility for compacting terminal progress while keeping idempotence receipts, and L3a-L1 proves the adapter policy on SQLite. Acceptance: bounded storage under prolonged flow, and replays and restarts that skip no work. This proof blocks R-S.
- [ ] State at S `bb3f3cefb` (added on 2026-11-19): `local-live` requires `atomic-units-v1`, `scope-outbox-v1`, `generations-v1`, `leased-snapshots-v1` and `fenced-writers-v1` (`packages/cluster-mesh/src/storage/profiles.ts:70-76`). The SQLite backend durably advertises `atomic-units-v1` and `immutable-chains-v1` only (`src/storage/sqlite/index.ts:60-63`); its journal lanes, fences, barriers and purge jobs are session-local (`:52-58`). The kernel refuses `generations-v1` and `leased-snapshots-v1` (`src/storage/scope-runtime.ts:61-62`) and no file implements them. Four profiles are therefore missing on SQLite, and the generation protocol is missing in the kernel. Lots K5, S1b, S2g and S3 below close that gap before L3a-L1 and L3a-L2; none is delivered. No sentropic plan document owns them (no tracked file there names S2g), so this plan does.

GUARD: `> a backend that has not qualified generations-v1 or leased-snapshots-v1 still refuses them explicitly`.
- [ ] RED, S1b (the part of SPEC_L3A §17.1 row S1, “journal and fences”, that is not durable at S `bb3f3cefb`): `packages/cluster-mesh/tests/storage/sqlite-journal.spec.ts > a committed segment and its fenced checkpoint survive close and reopen and replay is contiguous`; `packages/cluster-mesh/tests/storage/sqlite-fences.spec.ts > owner epoch incarnation and lease survive reopen and a stale writer fails after a lock wait and at commit`. GUARD: `sqlite-fences.spec.ts > scope-outbox-v1 and fenced-writers-v1 are advertised only after their durable corpus passes`.
- [ ] RED, S2g: `packages/cluster-mesh/tests/storage/sqlite-generations.spec.ts > the K5 generation corpus passes on real SQLite including the G0 G1 stale-base race`; `> a crash between seal and publish leaves the previous generation readable and the staged one invisible`; `> leased pins survive reopen and block disposal of their generation until release`; `packages/cluster-mesh/tests/storage/sqlite-scheduler.spec.ts > admitted hot work is not displaced repeatedly and historical work still progresses under one coordinated writer`. On the commissioning host the default `/usr/bin/node` v22.22.1 embeds SQLite 3.46.1 and fails the predicate, and v24.18.0 embeds 3.53.1 and passes (measured on 2026-11-19); the 22 line is not advertised for local SQLite on that evidence.

- [x] RED (review 6.1, HEAD/index/worktree union): `tests/live-worktree-safety.test.ts > HEAD-only paths removed from the index and ignored still pass attribute checks`.
- [x] RED (review 6.1, index-context attributes): `tests/live-worktree-safety.test.ts > staged attribute rules gate staged blob reads independently of the worktree copy`.

- [x] RED (review 6.1, byte-exact paths): `tests/live-worktree-safety.test.ts > non-UTF-8 and control-character paths survive byte-exactly`.
- [x] RED (review 6.1, absolute common dir): `tests/live-worktree-safety.test.ts > the common dir resolves absolutely for main linked and separate repositories`.
- [x] RED (review 6.1, dev/ino/ctime identity): `tests/live-worktree-safety.test.ts > a same-size same-mtime replacement is detected and retried`.
- [x] RED (review 6.1, real concurrency and held lock): `tests/live-worktree-safety.test.ts > captures overlap real index writers and an occupied index lock without blocking or mutation`.
- [x] RED (review 6.1, blob type/size before content): `tests/live-worktree-safety.test.ts > oversized staged blobs are excluded before any content read`.
- [x] RED (review sol2, descriptor-anchored read, D-6): `tests/live-worktree-safety.test.ts > a parent redirected during the open is rejected through the opened descriptor and the observation re-run`.
- [x] RED (review sol2, policy read through the safe reader): `tests/live-worktree-safety.test.ts > a policy file replaced between stat and read is never read through`.
- [x] RED (review sol2, exact hash-object form, no --filters reactivation): `tests/live-worktree-safety.test.ts > hash-object accepts only its exact raw form and a filter reactivator is refused`.
- [x] RED (review sol3, external attribute sources): `tests/live-worktree-safety.test.ts > repository-external attribute and config sources are never consulted by collector invocations`.
- [x] RED (review sol3, worktree policy before the staged blob): `tests/live-worktree-safety.test.ts > an attribute change during the collection loop stops the attempt before the newly forbidden file is read` (b.txt is now tracked, so its staged blob must never be read either).
- [x] RED (review sol4, local config includes): `tests/live-worktree-safety.test.ts > local config include directives are refused before Git loads them` (absolute and relative `include.path` toward a file outside the root, plus raw-written `includeIf` and uppercase forms) and `> an include wired into the config during the capture refuses the attempt`; both confirmed red by removing the gate calls, then green.
- Each review RED case was confirmed red without its fix by a point mutation, then green after the correction.
- [x] Implement (sol2): content reads verify the opened descriptor after the open (readlink `/proc/self/fd/<fd>` strictly under the authorized root by segments, fstat regular, dev/ino against the inventory; off Linux the capability is explicitly unavailable and the observation re-run), policy files (.gitattributes, info/attributes) are read through that same safe reader, and hash-object runs only in its exact form `hash-object --no-filters --stdin`.
- [x] Implement (sol3): every collector invocation neutralizes all attribute and configuration sources outside the repository (isolated HOME/XDG_CONFIG_HOME, `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM`/`GIT_ATTR_NOSYSTEM`, `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null`, `-c core.attributesFile=/dev/null`, scrubbed `GIT_ATTR_SOURCE`/config injection, per-invocation environment limited to `GIT_DIR`/`GIT_WORK_TREE`), and the worktree attribute policy is revalidated before every staged blob read.
- [x] RED (review sol5, include added after the round gate): `tests/live-worktree-safety.test.ts > an include added after a round's config gate rejects that snapshot and the capture re-runs` (a valid included configuration wired in after a round's probe, withdrawn before the next gate: the contaminated round is rejected and nothing from it is published).
- [x] RED (review sol5, ungated parent discovery): `tests/live-worktree-safety.test.ts > a root without its own .git entry is refused before any Git discovery invocation` (a directory inside a repository is refused with zero spawned invocations, so parent discovery never loads an ungated configuration).
- [x] GUARD (driver decision sol5, -c precedence): `tests/live-worktree-safety.test.ts > collector -c hardening keeps precedence over configuration pulled in by an include` (an included file re-points `core.attributesFile`/`core.excludesFile` outside and re-enables fsmonitor; the command-line `-c` values decide, textconv/filters stay refused by argument form).
- [x] Implement (sol4): the repository's local configuration files (common config, `config.worktree` when present) are located in Node before the first Git invocation — Git parses the local configuration during repository discovery, includes included, before any `--no-includes` policy of the command itself applies (verified on Git 2.53) — and gated by a probe run outside any repository against the named file only (`config --file <path> --no-includes --get-regexp ^include`): any include/includeIf directive, in any casing, pointing anywhere, refuses the capture. The gate re-runs at every snapshot round (a directive wired in mid-capture refuses the attempt), the manual git-dir/common-dir resolution must agree with rev-parse, and `core.excludesFile=/dev/null` joins `core.attributesFile=/dev/null` in the hardening prefix.
- [x] Implement (sol5, driver decision): every snapshot round also re-probes the gated configuration AT ITS END — include directives re-probed through the same out-of-repository `--file` form (an in-repository `config --local --no-includes` probe would itself follow includes during discovery, so the end probe reuses the `^include` regexp without repository context) plus a content fingerprint of `.git/config` and the local config files — and a root without its own readable `.git` entry is refused before any Git invocation. A changed fingerprint or any directive rejects the round and re-runs the observation (nothing collected against the changed configuration is published); a directive persisting into the next round is refused by the start gate. The `-c` hardening keeps command-line precedence over every included value meanwhile.
- [x] Implement: inventory-before-content, attribute checks, direct safe file/raw blob reads, no-filter fingerprints without object writes, before/after stability checks and no-optional-lock/no-fsmonitor/no-lazy-fetch invocation policy. Carry common-dir/checkout isolation and staged/unstaged distinction from GitNexus/CodeGraph; include sparse/skip-worktree, symlink/gitlink, binary, oversized and protected paths.
- Allowed (≤8): E:`engram-live/git-executor.ts`, E:`engram-live/worktree.ts`, E:`engram-live/attributes.ts`, E:`engram-live/filesystem.ts`, E:`tests/live-worktree-safety.test.ts`, E:`tests/helpers/live-process-trace.ts`, E:`tests/helpers/live-git-fixtures.ts`, this plan.
- Forbidden: converting `status`/working-tree diff before exclusions, `update-index`, `write-tree`, optional index locks, deleting `index.lock`, `hash-object -w`, implicit fetch or following a symlink outside the authorized root.
- Gate: tracer programs, including a fail-on-call executable named `python3` implemented without Python, receive zero collector invocations; concurrent scenario has zero collector-caused lock failures. Isolated index/ref/checkout digests remain unchanged by collector operations; unsafe capture never becomes stable.

## L5-P — Packaged Reused Extraction And Production Parse Adapter (R11/A6, R3/R5/R7/R9, A1/R13) — engram

- Ratification: **ratified by the conductor on 2026-10-15** under design amendment A6 and Opus review 2 minors 1–2. Implementation and qualification remain unchecked. Needs: L1b package boundary and L5 producer seams. L7b-R and R-E cannot consume installed L5 before this gate passes; R10/R1 remains a separate L5 acceptance requirement. The Allowed list ratified on 2026-10-15 is replaced by the 2026-11-19 split below, which takes effect with the validation of this amendment.
- 2026-11-19 split. The single 12-file lot could not pass its own gate: every new emitted file is rejected by the two packed-surface guard files, which were not in its Allowed list (`scripts/check-live-package.mjs:502-528`, `:556`; `tests/live-neutrality.test.ts:26-67`), lifting `unavailable` needs `engram-live/contracts.ts` (`:70-77`), and the reported L6b items m8 and m14 needed files it did not list. With them it reached 14 to 17 files. It is now five sub-lots, each within 12 files, in this order: **L5-P-m8**, **L5-P0**, **L5-P1**, **L5-P2**, **L5-D**. Every former RED and GUARD is kept and assigned below. L6b item m14 moves to E-ASM1.
- Round-2 split of L5-P2. Both round-2 validations found that L5-P2 could not pass its gate inside its Allowed list, for a cause that predates round 1: the import closure of the reused extraction was not accounted for. L5-P2 is now two sub-lots, **L5-P2a** (the closed core, in the root sources) and **L5-P2b** (its delivery in the package), which makes six sub-lots in this order: L5-P-m8, L5-P0, L5-P1, L5-P2a, L5-P2b, L5-D. The alternative offered by the Fable validation, a conductor decision that the production adapter of L5-P1 replaces R11 for commissioning, is not taken here: it would withdraw an obligation ratified on 2026-10-15 (A6) and stated by LIVE §11, which a plan author cannot do.
- Closure of the reused extraction, measured at `f75543a5` (round 2; value imports, read in the sources). The lot ratified on 2026-10-15 named one obstacle, the `web-tree-sitter` import. The static closure holds six edges that the delivered guards refuse. (1) `src/extract.ts:24` imports the bare module `web-tree-sitter` and uses its values: `Parser` (`:28-31`, `:51`) and `Language.load` (`:358`, `:369`). (2) `src/extract.ts:11` imports `createRequire` from `node:module` and evaluates it when the module loads (`:47`), for the legacy grammar lookup (`:99`) and the non-live `extends` resolution (`:637-649`). (3) `src/extract.ts:18` imports `./detect.js` for `shebangInterpreter`, used on the non-live branch only (`:272`); `src/detect.ts` reaches `./extract-git.js`, `./google-workspace.js` and `./office-guard.js`. (4) `src/extract.ts:19` imports `./relation-remap.js`, used on the live path (`:7142`, `:7167`, `:7171`); `src/relation-remap.ts:1` imports `./graph.js` for the pure function `assertionIdentityOf` (`src/graph.ts:46-52`), and `src/graph.ts:1` imports the bare module `graphology`. (5) `src/extract.ts:17` imports `./security.js` for `sanitizeLabel`, used once, by the MCP-configuration extractor (`:6921`); `src/security.ts` reaches `./paths.js`, `node:dns/promises` and `node:net`. (6) `src/extract.ts:15` imports six values from `./cache.js`; `src/cache.ts` takes the source-root cache directory from `./paths.js` (`:17`, `:38`), writes files, registers a process exit hook (`:56`) and imports `./graph-memory-projection.js` (`:18`), which reaches `./graph.js` and the built output of `engram-memory` (`src/graph-memory-projection.ts:33`). `src/node-id.ts` has no import. Three constraints close the obvious routes: the package project has `rootDir: "."` (`engram-live/tsconfig.json:13`) and the root project has `rootDir: "./src"` (`tsconfig.json:8`), so neither compiles the other's sources; every `.ts` file under `engram-live/` is scanned as package source and may not import `../src/` (Commissioning Rules, packed-surface rule); and no emitted file may name a bare module, `node:module` or a loader factory (`scripts/check-live-package.mjs:431-470`).
- Technique (round 2). **L5-P2a** cuts the six edges inside the root sources and changes no packed file. The deterministic extraction moves into a closed core module, `src/extract-core.ts`. The static value-import closure of that core holds only `node:` builtins other than `node:module`, `src/node-id.ts`, `src/relation-remap.ts` and a new leaf, `src/live-cache-keys.ts`; type-only imports are erased at emit and do not count. `src/extract.ts` keeps its path and every export name: it becomes the root host module, which imports the core, re-exports it, and binds the root-only capabilities into it as host defaults: the tree-sitter runtime (edge 1), the Node module resolver (edge 2), `shebangInterpreter` (edge 3), `sanitizeLabel` (edge 5) and the file cache of the root product, which is the filesystem branch of `loadCached` and `saveCached` (edge 6). No importer of `src/extract.ts` changes. Edge 4 is cut by moving `assertionIdentityOf` into `src/relation-remap.ts`, which then imports types only; `src/graph.ts` imports the function from there and re-exports it, so it keeps one definition and no importer of `./graph.js` changes. Edge 6 is cut in two parts; the second was added in round 3 (Fable R3-m2). First, the live key and bind functions the core uses (`liveExtractionKey`, `liveExtractionBind`, `LIVE_EXTRACTOR_VERSION`, `assertValidLiveStore` and their types) move into `src/live-cache-keys.ts`, which `src/cache.ts` re-exports under the same names. Second, the envelope protocol of the injected store moves into the closed set with them, as one definition that `src/cache.ts` reuses and that adds no module to the set. Today the live path reaches the injected store through `loadCached` and `saveCached` themselves (`src/extract.ts:7567`, `:7597`), and their injected branch is that protocol (`src/cache.ts:519-570`, `:654-696`): key and bind check, payload digest, refusal of a conflicting payload, relativization of source paths. That branch also calls the memory-freshness gate (`src/cache.ts:564`, `:670`), which belongs to `src/graph-memory-projection.ts`, a module outside the closed set: the gate stays in the root wrappers `loadCached` and `saveCached`, and the closed set refuses a memory-carrying payload, which it neither serves nor stores, without importing the projection module. On the live path the core therefore consults no host default: a live operation takes its bytes from the injected read port, its grammars from the injected grammar port, and its cache from the injected store through the protocol of the closed set. An extractor that is not behind an advertised grammar (project files, MCP configuration, regex-backed templates) stays a root capability and is explicitly unavailable in the neutral build. **L5-P2b** delivers that core in the package with the repository's TypeScript compiler alone: no bundler and no new build dependency. `scripts/build-live.mjs` holds the single enumeration of the closed set, runs a second compiler pass over it and installs the emitted JavaScript under `dist/reused/`, with no declaration file for those modules. The in-package entry `engram-live/reused-extraction.ts` imports `./reused/extract-core.js`; at compile time that import is typed by the committed declaration `engram-live/reused/extract-core.d.ts`, and the build fails with `OUTPUT_INVALID` when the emitted core does not export every name the declaration declares. The entry receives the parser runtime through the `GrammarRuntimePort` as extended by L5-P1, binds it as the only host capability of the core, and always runs the core in live mode.
- Parser runtime rule (one definition): the neutral package declares no dependency and ships no parser runtime. The tree-sitter runtime reaches the package only through the host-injected `GrammarRuntimePort` (`engram-live/integration.ts:171-174`), as the installed-package test already does (`tests/live-package.test.ts:220`, `:245`). The former instruction to package the web-tree-sitter runtime is withdrawn: the delivered checker forbids any dependency and any bare import (`scripts/check-live-package.mjs:469`, `:569-571`, `:592-594`). The packaged assets are the pinned grammar WASM files.

The test imports the core directly, injects the parser runtime explicitly, as the package entry will, and replaces every other host default by a stub that counts its calls and throws. It runs the same extraction twice on one injected store and asserts three things: the first run stores an envelope through the injected store, the second run is a warm hit served from it, and every stub has recorded zero call. It proves at run time, not only by a static scan, that the live path consults no host default. The count is required (Fable R3-m2): the two cache calls of the live path sit in `try` blocks (`src/extract.ts:7566-7570`, `:7596-7600`), so a stub that only throws still lets the extraction complete, and the round-2 form of this case, titled “… completes with every root host default replaced by a throwing stub”, would have passed with the injected store left behind a host default.

The reused extraction uses nothing else of the runtime (`Parser`, `Language.load`, and the `Node` and `Tree` types; `src/extract.ts:25-31`, `:51`, `:358`, `:369`). - [x] Implement, L5-P2a: build the closed core by the technique above. The first act of the branch runs the closure audit on its base and records the measured edges; if the cut needs a root module that the Allowed list lacks, the lot stops before any code is written. Since round 3 the list is at the 12-file limit, so the former room for two more files is gone: such a module splits the lot under the rule of the Commissioning Rules (a list above 12 files is split before any code is written, and no gate is dropped by a split). The L5 seam audit of the build and its test (`scripts/build-live.mjs:141-165`; `tests/live-package.test.ts:695`) follow the seam markers to the files that now carry them, and the test keeps its title in this lot. The lot emits and packs no new file, changes no capability and adds no fact kind.
- [x] Implement, L5-P2a (round 3, writer inventory; Fable R3-m1): the repository inventories every edge-row constructor and raw edge rewrite under `src/` and requires an allowance for each (`tests/multi-links-acceptance.test.ts:169-177`); an allowance matches on file, owner, kind and fingerprint (`tests/helpers/multi-links-inventory.ts:139`). The code that moves to `src/extract-core.ts` carries allowances keyed to `src/extract.ts`: the Fable round-3 validation counted 43 of the 454 entries of `tests/fixtures/multi-links/writer-allowlist.json` (38 edge-row constructors, 4 raw edge rewrites and 1 raw boundary, whose counts sum to 50), and one raw-boundary tuple of the helper names `src/extract.ts` with `_mergeSwiftExtensions` (`multi-links-inventory.ts:126`). The lot moves each allowance with its code. A moved allowance changes its `file` and `line` fields only, so that an unchanged `owner`, `kind`, `fingerprint` and `count` proves a verbatim move, and the helper tuple follows its function to the new file; otherwise that boundary check stops matching without failing. The two allowances of `src/cache.ts` that cover `relativizeSourceFilesIn` and `absolutizeSourceFilesIn` move in the same way with the envelope protocol. No allowance is added for a new site and none is widened: a site that needs a new fingerprint is a change of behaviour, which this lot does not make.

Add the root files of the closed set to the freshness inputs of the test bootstrap (`tests/live-neutrality.test.ts:26-40`), so that a change of the core rebuilds the package. The delivered test `L5 source audit and packaged grammar inventory; reused-extraction delivery remains unverified (A6)` is renamed `L5 source audit and packaged grammar inventory` by this lot, which delivers what its title calls unverified; this plan records the rename. The installed result resolves entirely inside its tarball. Round 3: the warm hit that its second case needs is served by the envelope protocol of the closed set (L5-P2a); the neutral entry binds the parser runtime only, and the neutral build holds no cache host default. The lot changes no capability (`code-analysis` is available since L5-P1 and the list keeps ten entries), so `contracts.ts` is not in its list; it adds no fact kind.

It emits no new file and changes no capability.

That suite belongs to `npm test` (`vitest.config.ts:11`, `package.json:81`), which the hosted lane runs (`.github/workflows/typescript-ci.yml:40`), so without this command the failure would appear only on the hosted lane, after the code is written. Refuse root fallback and execute the installed R13 transitive closure scan. The producer side of m8 and all of m14 belong to step 1 and precede R-E-pre; the storage proofs of m8 and m12 on SQLite follow R-E-pre and block R-E and R-S.

Les deux typechecks prescrits et le typecheck strict supplémentaire des quatre modules L6b et des trois fichiers de tests/helper passent. La revue Opus a2 finale est ACCEPTABLE, zéro majeur, quatre mineurs corrigés ci-dessous sur décision du conducteur, sans nouveau tour ; aucune acceptation S/H ni publication R-E n'est revendiquée.
- Reproductions : cinq échecs constatés sur le code reçu (projection ancienne après observation silencieuse, autorité absente, preuve absente, deux soumissions conflictuelles annoncées réussies, admission backfill à 64 Kio). Deux autres échecs ont été reproduits puis corrigés pendant la reprise : observation en panne effacée et compteur de tentatives réinitialisé avec un nouveau gestionnaire. Les anciens cas déjà verts sont conservés comme gardes et discriminés par mutation.
- Propriétés : source et projection distinctes, suppressions et réparation des relations, graines rejouables et réduction gloutonne, frontières asynchrones suspendues, 10 003 chemins au-delà du plafond d'un tour, 2 000 résultats avec voisin suspendu, 10 000 appels identiques, créations/suppressions répétées. Compteurs indépendants des ports et tailles réelles des collections du gestionnaire ; observation du GC : `not covered`, aucune qualification RAM/backend/hôte déduite de ces doubles.
- Mutations avant la revue a1 : **41/41 tuées**, campagne également rejouée par le relecteur ; restauration des sources vérifiée par SHA-256 après chaque essai. Matrice historique `L6B_MUTATIONS` dans `tests/helpers/live-clock-watch.ts`. Ce résultat ne qualifie pas la présente correction : la nouvelle exclusivité d'écrivain peut rendre redondante une garde de sérialisation. La preuve actuelle est le gate ci-dessus et les régressions directes décrites ci-dessous ; aucune nouvelle campagne exhaustive de mutation n'est revendiquée.

| Classe / revues couvertes | Construction et mutations discriminantes |

- Limites enregistrées avant exécution (profil factice, sans assouplir LIVE §13) : 64 unités admises / 16 Mio résidents, 4 opérations globales et 1 par scope, 4 enfants Git, fichier 1 Mio + marge 64 octets, batch 1 Mio (plafond fixe conservé ≤4 Mio), découverte 8 Kio, réservation producteur 4 Mio, 4 chemins/page et 2 pages/tour, 64 tours, 16 deltas/chemins watch, chemin ≤1 024 octets, debounce 500 ms, polling autonome 10 000 ms, 2 retries, backoff ≤30 s, échéance tentative 30 s / annulation 10 ms, 32 consommateurs, 8 diagnostics de 64 caractères et quota durable 100 000. Les tests varient capacités, priorités et scopes ; le corpus de 10 003 chemins utilise 64 chemins/page et 4 pages/tour.
- Contrat requis pour S : incarnation possédée par le stockage, existence des générations, reçus et progrès durables, quota et fencing atomique sur chaque mutation. Le helper l'applique ; aucune implémentation DB/hôte ni qualification de performance n'est revendiquée en E. Aucun historique Git n'est élagué.
- Correction MAJEUR A/B3 : époque de cycle de vie capturée avant la microtâche, garde avant tout tour/acquisition, état `stopping` irréversible, révocation synchrone du bail avant toute attente ou appel d'horloge, contrôle du jeton au récepteur de publication, nouvelle garde avant l'affectation des certificats et axes non courants après l'arrêt. RED reproduit sur `bootstrap()` immédiatement suivi de `shutdown()`, puis vert pour les trois entrées bootstrap/round/converge. Arrêt injecté avant/après chaque appel de port observé d'un tour initial et d'un tour delta (collecte, réparation, validation, publication, marqueurs, progrès, acquisition et horloge) : projection et marqueurs inchangés après l'arrêt, aucune certification, réservations libérées. Un test de publication ignore volontairement le signal d'annulation et reste refusé par le bail révoqué.

|---|---|
| m1 | Corrigé : identifiants annulés conservés puis `discardPaused` après quiescence ; travail en file à l'arrêt testé avec `workReservedBytes === 0`. Un bail révoqué ne tente aucun nouveau reçu de parking. |

| m4 | Corrigé : enregistrement sous le ledger avec résidents + plus grande réservation producteur ≤ capacité interactive hors réserve backfill, y compris pour les inscriptions suivantes. Sondage et certification réservent au plus 64 Kio. Quatre scopes simultanés convergent ; l'inscription suivante infaisable est refusée. |
| m5 | Corrigé : le tick valide d'abord et ne crée plus de doute ou de passe complète au repos ; un écart de preuve déclenche la réparation. Corpus de 2 000 fichiers : trois ticks calmes sans passe, puis delta chaud certifié au tick suivant. |

Tout adaptateur nécessitant une lecture réelle doit la faire sous permis dans les ports asynchrones, avant de fournir l'instantané. |
| m7 | Corrigé : séquence propre au dernier débordement capturée au début de la passe ; un changement connu ne devient plus un nouveau débordement. Modification sur la dernière page : une seule passe, débordement acquitté, delta réparé. Un nouveau débordement reste invalidant. |

Les gardes redondantes ne sont pas présentées comme mutations nécessairement discriminantes. |

Aucune preuve de collecteur installé déduite du rig. |

|---|---|
| n1 | Identité de page complète liée à son empreinte ; conflit résiduel transformé en doute inconnu et nouvelle passe sans parking d'échec. Un échec non relançable arrête le drive courant. Régressions rouges puis vertes : réparation partielle puis modification avec/sans signal, conflit injecté, convergence et quota durable inchangé ; erreur permanente appelée une seule fois au lieu de 64. |
| n2 | Époque capturée par `drive()` transmise à `round()`, `collectDeltas()` et chaque `work()`, contrôlée avant acquisition et après résultat ; certification liée à cette même époque. Régression rouge puis verte : annulation à chaque microtâche du tour complet (scan initial de trois pages, delta, débordement, tick périodique), aucune collecte/publication/mutation ultérieure ni axe `current` pendant ou après l'annulation, réservations libérées et reprise convergente. |
| n3 | Jeton revérifié avant parking d'échec durable ; jeton révoqué → résultat `fenced` sans appel au parking. Le faux `park` refuse aussi les jetons invalides. Régression rouge puis verte avec révocation dans le worker suivie d'une erreur générique. |
| n4 | Reports m8 et m14 matérialisés en cases ouvertes bloquant R-E et dans L5-P ; m12 matérialisé dans L3a/L3b-S. Raisons, lots cibles et critères d'acceptation conservés ci-dessus ; ces travaux restent ouverts et hors implémentation L6b. |

- Gate final après corrections a2 (2026-10-16) : `npx vitest run tests/live-reconcile.test.ts tests/live-scheduler-budget.test.ts tests/live-worktree-safety.test.ts tests/live-neutrality.test.ts tests/live-package.test.ts` — **90/90 tests verts, cinq fichiers, zéro skip** (60 tests L6b). `npx tsc --noEmit -p .` et `npx tsc --noEmit -p engram-live/tsconfig.json` verts ; typecheck strict NodeNext supplémentaire des quatre modules L6b et des trois fichiers de tests/helper vert ; `git diff --check` propre. Pression temporaire d'admission `capacity` conservée relançable pour que les quatre scopes admis convergent ; les échecs terminaux arrêtent le drive. Le fichier généré `src/workspace/tokens-st-css.generated.ts` est inchangé.
- Empreinte finale du code/tests après corrections a2 : SHA-256 `fd7cca43fe37e985070014cbeb5f7262af97789a758439d04b9f5813de2c7f2a`, même convention des huit fichiers triés, plan exclu. Exécution offline avec outils installés, aucun Python, réseau, installation ni push ; uniquement les neuf chemins Allowed L6b modifiés. Un seul commit L6b autorisé par le conducteur ; aucun nouveau tour de revue.
