An absent input, a mismatch or an unstable capture (`UnstableCapture`, `:119-124`) keeps `unstable-capture` exactly as today. The default does not change: a call with `treeOid === null` and no verified-capture input still returns `partial` with the reason `unstable-capture`, so that the delivered case `Opus2 local capture reports unstable-capture for content %j` (`tests/live-code-version.test.ts:1506`) stays green with no change to that file, which is not in the list. `engram-live/worktree.ts` is not edited: the factory composes what the capture already returns. `code-analysis.ts` is already packed, so `factory-local` remains the only emitted module this lot adds.

A code anchor adds the algorithm-qualified commit OID, the path bytes in the lossless encoding of the delivered contracts, the blob OID, an inclusive one-based line range (`LineRangeDto`, `engram-live/contracts.ts:115`) and, when it is a line origin, the calculation version of the line attribution. A commit anchor adds the algorithm-qualified commit OID. A PR anchor adds the GitHub repository identity, the PR number and the pinned head and base observation identities. Keys are exact and an extra key is refused. An anchor holds no memory identifier, citation text, reason text or session content: it is a reference that the S reader joins to memory reads under their own authorization.

**Pinned case corpus (analysis C).** Aliases refer to the inspected local Git objects, not whatever `origin/main` later names: **E**=`545a57b8`, **E0**=`e432240f`; **H**=`029e8138`, **HP**=`8d4b1e46d`, **HR**=`4fab7472e`; **S**=`fb5e92a6`, **K3**=`534922e70`, **K4**=`b789caa4c`. Expand every alias and case-specific OID to its full algorithm-qualified OID in the manifest before measurement. Expected answers and authorized local captures remain `unverified` until prepared and independently checked. E/H/S here identify revisions; path prefixes elsewhere still identify repositories.

| No. | Real question | Independent expected control |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | E: which commit introduced `delivered: false` in `engram-live/integration.ts`? | Exact Git attribution, line and OID. |
| 2 | E: when and how did the implicit 200-commit cap disappear from `src/extract-git.ts`? | History, patch and actual scope of the change. |
| 3 | Where was agent-stats in E0, why did it disappear in E, and where is it in H? | Git trees and evidence of transfer to `packages/agent-stats`. |
| 4 | What changed in `engram-live/` between E0 and E? | Exhaustive file list and differences at both pinned endpoints. |
| 5 | How did `compareBranches` evolve through E to verify every merge-base? | Symbol versions, patches, tests and declared limits. |
| 6 | Why does `ingestActivity` produce pending candidates rather than admitted memories? | Code and decision D-8 A; no automatic admission. |
| 7 | What is the latest authorized index/worktree state of the declared fixture worktree `F_WT`? (Re-pinned on 2026-11-19: the former target, the worktree `graphify-wt-livegit`, was removed after its branch merged.) | Identified stable capture or explicit absence/refusal; no reconstruction of local-state history. `F_WT` is built and removed by the harness with a recorded recipe, full OIDs and permissions; it is never an owner worktree. |
| 8 | H: where did `worktree add --detach … HEAD` in `resolveJobCwd` come from? | Attribution/history; a reason only if documented. |
| 9 | What does HP add to H for GitHub PRs, and what remains outside that transport? | Delta distinguishing metadata from object acquisition. |
| 10 | What does HR add to retain objects after branch deletion, and is it integrated in H? | Delta, ancestry and qualification status; no claim of an unexecuted acceptance test. |
| 11 | Why does `live-registration.ts` deny local authority by default? | Code, verified-principal requirement and L7a dependency. |
| 12 | Why does `recallMemory` distinguish refusal from an empty list? | Consumer contract, provenance and actual integration state. |
| 13 | Do HP and HR modify common paths or contracts? | Contributions from their respective bases; overlap distinguished from conflict. |
| 14 | In the 90 days before H, which tests most often changed with `delegate.ts`? | Reproducible counts, denominator and merge-counting rule; optional G5 absence stays explicit. |
| 15 | S: which commit introduced `POLICY_VERSION` in `live-storage/registration.ts`? | Attribution and contract version. |
| 16 | Which change made removal retryable in that file? | History, patch and proof associated with `fb8576d4`. |
| 17 | Between K3 and K4, which files and visibility behaviors were added around `ScopeVisibilityRoot`? | Diff/code; development branch distinguished from main. |
| 18 | Why does the reusable Postgres backend belong to cluster-mesh? | L3a v4.1, superseded passage and remaining app responsibilities. |
| 19 | Which h2a/sentropic consumers of `WorkspaceDeclarationDto` are resolved, copied or still undetermined? | Repository/version provenance; no automatic identification of copied contracts. |
| 20 | Can an agent without authorization for its provider obtain unpublished local sentropic content? | Refusal before emission; neither content nor derivatives disclosed. |

**Protocol fixed before execution (analysis C, owner decisions applied).**

1. **T0 baseline:** the currently available workflow, including Git, engram tools, document search and memory that is actually accessible. Record tool versions and permissions; do not reduce the baseline to Git alone.
2. **T1 native read side:** the same 20 questions, rights and pinned versions with live readers. Record prototype versus installed delivery and coverage. The native choice replaces the analysis's proposed git-ai variants.
3. **Gain attribution:** also time the service limited to already planned lots/readers. Report that improvement separately from the incremental effect of G1–G5, including whether G5 was implemented.
4. **Timing:** question received → usable, verified answer, including searches, mistakes, recovery and retries. Counterbalance T0/T1 order between operators and hide the expected answers. Publish first answers and warm repetitions separately; report bootstrap, CPU/RSS and storage separately, with commands, hardware and artifact identities.
5. **Correctness:** verify OIDs, paths, ranges, ordered parents, generations and citations against independent evidence. Reasons require explicit sources; known gaps must be stated. Failed cases stay in the corpus and cannot satisfy constant-quality acceptance. **Gain of a non-success case (one rule, fixed on 2026-11-19):** when the T1 arm ends without the verified answer (refusal, denial, absent capability or capture, unavailable policy, failure), the operator recovers through the T0 workflow, and T1ᵢ is the time to that non-success plus T0ᵢ. The gain is then −(time to non-success)/T0ᵢ: it is never positive, and the case remains a failed case. When the expected answer is itself a refusal (case 20), a correct refusal is a success and is timed normally. When neither arm reaches the verified answer, the gain is 0 and the case remains failed. **Baseline arm that fails (round 2):** a baseline time cap C, the same for the 20 cases, is recorded in the manifest before any measurement and is never changed afterwards. A T0 arm that has not reached the verified answer at C stops there and is scored T0ᵢ = C; when the T1 arm reaches the verified answer, the gain is 1 − T1ᵢ/C, and the case counts as answered by the native service only. Every case therefore has a numeric gain; case 14 has one whether or not the optional G5 exists.
6. **Continuation:** primary score is `median(1 − T1ᵢ/T0ᵢ)` on all **20 first investigations**, at constant quality; continue if **≥30%**. The decision needs exactly 20 numeric gains; otherwise it is `not-computable`. Never remove a failed case or retune the threshold after seeing results. No benchmark has run in this documentation amendment; all gains are `unverified`.
7. **Agent execution:** a separate pilot is conditional on **≥20% additional gain** versus read-only service, with preparation, copy, verification, recovery and cleanup included. It adds no execution lot to plan/09 and waives no confidentiality/reliability gate.

## Out Of Scope

- [ ] Direct database-to-Git synchronization or any file generated by live for version control, including the older proposed bounded JSON export.
- [ ] Global migration of documentary corpora, annotations or canonical agent-memory stores; changes to memory tombstones/raw-SQL policy.
- [ ] LLM ranking, embeddings, assistant work emission, upstream runtime adoption, implementation of additional graph engines in this delivery, general remote refresh or implicit telemetry. The port must remain suitable for later graph backends.
- [ ] A standalone engram daemon independent of cluster-mesh; registration triggered by every agent session; unqualified non-Linux/runtime/platform support.
- [ ] Commits, Track imports, pushes, merges, package publication or product deployment in the S0 authoring turn.

## Feedback Loop

- [ ] The conductor owns cross-repository coordination, commits and Track imports; S0 performs none. Each receiving lot records its exact repository/branch, file scope and dependency evidence before implementation.
- [ ] Capture the actual failing baseline before marking a proposed case RED. If it already passes, relabel it GUARD and record a discriminating mutation for a critical guard. Manual S0 document checks do not masquerade as executable tests.
- [ ] Preserve completed evidence separately from planned obligations; future lots remain unchecked until their gates run. Missing required backend/runtime infrastructure is a failed gate, never a silent skip.
- [ ] L3a records D/Q7 outcomes against fixed LIVE §13 thresholds. Measured failure activates the already accepted applicable B alternative after conductor review, with impact/rollback and requalification evidence; no runtime fallback and no owner re-poll unless the alternative violates an accepted constraint.
- [ ] Carry R-E → R-C → R-S → R-H release evidence between repositories; h2a must update its exact cluster-mesh pin. No package publication or plan import occurs in S0.
- [ ] Opus S0 findings are mapped in LIVE's final disposition table; all implementation/runtime/performance gates remain `unverified`. The base is two commits behind the inspected `origin/main`; neither commit touches the four S0 documents. Rebase belongs to later PR preparation, not this uncommitted authoring pass.
- [ ] Each carried behavior in LIVE §15 and each upstream parity row in §14 maps to the named lot/case; reconciliation removes unsupported claims rather than dropping coverage silently. Conductor review and product activation remain distinct from S0 document completion.
- [ ] 2026-11-19: no builder is assigned before both validators return GO on this amended plan and on LIVE. The conductor then records the GO, imports the Commissioning Order into Track (Track held no item for this chain on 2026-11-19, according to the Fable validation) and assigns row 1. A builder's first evidence line states its repository, branch, base commit, exact Allowed list, disk class and measured free space.
- [ ] 2026-11-19: release evidence follows R-E-pre → R-E → R-C → R-S → R-H as defined in the Commissioning Rules; a checkpoint is never inferred from a merged lot.
- [ ] 2026-11-19, round 2: the chain continues with R-S2 → R-H2 before the installed run of L9-H. R-E is recorded as a re-pack with its file-by-file comparison, never as the same digest as R-E-pre. The conductor acts added by round 2 are: the disk reclaim recorded before row 14, the baseline time cap recorded before the first timing sample, the approval of the Postgres 16 image fetch for the Core gate, and the four full-corpus runs (Q0, Q0-P, Q, L9-H) when the class D floor holds.
- [ ] 2026-11-19, round 2: the amendment that answers the round-2 validations is not a GO. No builder is assigned before both validators return GO on the amended plan and on LIVE; a third round can be limited to L5-P2a, L5-P2b, L5-P-m8, E-ASM1 and the lines listed in the round-2 ledger.
- [ ] 2026-11-19, round 3 (final fold): the owner decided GO with the correction and the cleanup. That decision is the GO; it supersedes the condition of the two items above, which required a GO from both validators. Carbon stated that its verdict converts to GO once R3-01 is applied by its fix, and that fix is applied as written; the GO-WITH-MINORS of Fable assumed R3-m1 and R3-m2 written before row 7a is assigned, and both are applied. Neither validator has re-read this fold, and the owner decision asks for no fourth round: the conductor checks the fold against the two round-3 files, runs the documentary gate (`git diff --check`, exactly two changed paths), records the GO with the heads E `f751f8d6`, S `bb3f3cefb` and H `e9ce1a8db`, imports the Commissioning Order into Track with its Builder column, and assigns row 0b, then each row whose predecessors are met.

### Packed-surface audit of every E lot

The conductor design note of round 2 names a defect class seen in round 1: a lot sized without the packed-surface guards. The rule of the Commissioning Rules is applied here to every E lot of the order. “Run” means that the lot runs the two guards in its gate without listing them, because it adds no emitted file.

| Row | Lot | New emitted or packed file | Guard files | Capability change and `contracts.ts` | Fact kind | Files | Amended in round 2 |

## Validation r3 — dispositions

The third validations of this plan and of LIVE, as amended in round 2 and uncommitted in the worktree `graphify-wt-golive`, were returned on 2026-11-19, in the same ignored design directory as rounds 1 and 2: `VALIDATE-golive-fable-r3.md` (Fable 5.2: GO-WITH-MINORS; no blocker, no major, 2 minors and 3 observations) and `VALIDATE-golive-carbon-r3.md` (Carbon: NO-GO; 1 major, R3-01, and no blocker; its report lists no minor). The owner then decided GO with the correction and the cleanup, and asked for a builder per lot by difficulty (header, round-3 amendment). One line per finding follows. “Applied” means that this plan was amended; it never means that a code or test gate passed. Anchors written `plan/09:N` in the validations refer to this file as amended in round 2 (1,164 lines). Method of the author: as in round 2 the shell could not start in the authoring session (read-only filesystem for its working directory), so this plan, LIVE, both validations and the engram sources of the worktree, whose `HEAD` is `f75543a5`, were read through the file tools; no compiler, test, build or `git` command was executed, and the documentary gate of this fold is run by the conductor. The source anchors that this fold adds were re-read there: `engram-live/code-analysis.ts:137-153`, `:223`, `:240-245` and `:341-342`; `engram-live/worktree.ts:104-124`; `tests/live-code-version.test.ts:1506`; `tests/helpers/multi-links-inventory.ts:126` and `:139`; `tests/multi-links-acceptance.test.ts:169-177`; `src/extract.ts:7566-7570` and `:7596-7600`; `src/cache.ts:519-570` and `:654-696`; `vitest.config.ts:11`; `package.json:81`; `.github/workflows/typescript-ci.yml:40`; `tests/live-neutrality.test.ts:945`. S and H were not read: every figure about them, the allowance counts of R3-m1 and the disk readings are taken from the validations and attributed to them.
